# Implementation log

## 2026-10-05 — Initial audit

- Verified the Git root, branch, remote, initial clean worktree, project layout, dependencies, model/data/evaluation code, and attached paper before model edits.
- Read all eight candidate HDF5 files read-only and scanned numeric datasets in chunks. Recorded shapes, types, attributes, finite checks, label counts, available identifiers, and the unresolved 5G CSI provenance question in `02_dataset_inventory.md`.
- Documented the prose/Table I conflicts and the existing pretraining/downstream gaps in `01_repository_and_paper_audit.md`.

## 2026-10-05 — Implementation and environment

- Added selectable official Mamba-2 and retained the ViT encoder, patch projection, positions, channel-major token order, predictor, target LayerNorm, mask indexing, and JEPA loss.
- Corrected per-item channel sampling to the paper's 1..C range; made target state/name checks and EMA handle named parameters and buffers; kept the target frozen and in evaluation mode.
- Added label-free pretraining, unlabeled per-source validation, gradient accumulation, finite loss/gradient checks, resume-state capture, graceful epoch-boundary stop, best/latest/periodic checkpoints, JSONL metrics, run metadata, dry-run, and one-step smoke mode.
- Replaced the broken old pretraining import path with a compatible forwarder; cleaned model/config package exports that referenced absent modules; retained a clear error for unsupported legacy WavesFM selection.
- Pinned Torch 2.10 CUDA 13, official Mamba source commit `e9594ce1c732d97440f0332fdc43170a2294dbfa`, and a matching official causal-conv1d 1.7.0 wheel. The first Mamba source install attempt exposed a missing `causal-conv1d` runtime call; adding the upstream wheel resolved it without an `nvcc` build.
- Added prose-default Mamba/ViT configs, a Table I alternative, and a bounded smoke config. Added shared-index frozen target linear probes and a comparison plot command.

## 2026-10-05 — Tests and GPU preflight

- Verified Python 3.13.15, uv 0.12.18, Torch 2.10.0+cu130, CUDA 13.0, RTX 5070 Ti (compute capability 12.0, 16,303 MiB), driver 610.60, absent `nvcc`, and reported bfloat16 support.
- Official Mamba-2 standalone CUDA forward/backward passed with finite output and input gradient.
- `uv run pytest -q`: **12 passed**. Tests exercise shape/finite outputs, direction alignment, variable channels and valid lengths, independent samples, target state/EMA, target indexing, checkpoint round-trip, the ViT path, actual HDF5-to-JEPA backward, and shared downstream split reuse.
- Four-source Mamba and ViT prose-default dry runs passed with CUDA predictor preflight shape `(48, 28, 256)`. Their inference preflight peaks were 361,396,224 and 98,454,016 bytes respectively.
- Batch-8 NRPos Indoor HDF5 smoke passed one full-precision optimizer update, finite loss, nonzero student/predictor gradients, a changed target parameter after EMA momentum 0.9, and checkpoint save. Peak PyTorch allocated memory was 1,407,824,896 bytes.
- Actual prose-config counts were 19,085,440 total / 9,908,928 trainable for Mamba-2 and 13,752,064 total / 7,242,240 trainable for ViT. The Mamba bidirectional configuration is not parameter-count matched.
- No full pretraining epoch/run and no downstream evaluation were launched. Generated smoke/preflight artifacts are under the sibling `../runs/latent-wave/` directory, outside the Git root.
- The test run emitted seven deprecation warnings from TorchMetrics, TileLang, and QuACK/CUTLASS dependencies; no test failed.

## 2026-10-05 — Full-run launch preparation

- The user authorized the full experiment after the earlier prepare-only objective. Confirmed branch `master`, HEAD `330e9ef5f6eb69e728ba43b3755036cffdd086cd`, and preserved the pre-existing staged implementation. No other GPU compute process was present; the RTX 5070 Ti reported 16,303 MiB and the sibling run volume had approximately 655 GB free.
- Re-read the data inventory and runbook. Both prose configs use only spectrogram, RFP, WiFi CSI, and NRPos Indoor; every configured HDF5 file/key exists. `5g-csi.h5` remains excluded. HDF5 access remains read-only.
- Added supported `--seed`, in-epoch foreground progress records, epoch ETA/throughput fields, cumulative resume elapsed time, and architecture/seed/config/Git revision/epoch/step/optimizer/scheduler metadata in checkpoints. Resume now rejects architecture or seed mismatches.
- Made full accumulation windows exact: incomplete loader batches are dropped and the last incomplete accumulation window is trimmed. Each optimizer update therefore uses 8 microbatches × 4 sources × 8 examples = 256 examples; the exact per-epoch step count is recorded.
- Pretraining, evaluation, and summary CLIs were inspected with `--help`. Config parity checks passed for data, masks, predictor, optimizer/schedule, epoch count, and seed. Latest-code CUDA dry-runs passed for both architectures (`cuda:0`, output `(48, 28, 256)`).
- Focused follow-up suite: `uv run pytest -q tests/test_pretrain_progress.py tests/test_mamba2_jepa.py::test_jepa_loss_gradients_optimizer_then_ema_and_checkpoint_resume` — **4 passed**; Python compilation and `git diff --check` passed.
- Full Mamba-2/ViT jobs have not completed an epoch yet. Exact run status, commands, logs, and measurements are tracked in `07_training_and_results.md` as the sequential runs proceed.

## 2026-10-07 — Read-only live monitor

- Inspected both requested console logs. Their `[progress]` JSON records include global step/total steps/total epochs, rolling loss, previous validation loss, LR, EMA, elapsed time/ETA, and allocated/reserved GPU bytes; `[epoch]` records contain final epoch metrics.

It follows the logs every two seconds, derives within-epoch progress from the logged global step/counts, keeps completed epoch summaries above a six-line dashboard, and checks `/proc` for live trainer state. It writes no training log, config, checkpoint, or data file.

- The future training logger change and smoke run are deferred until the current training runs finish, as requested; no active training process was stopped, restarted, or modified.

## 2026-10-07 — Validation reproducibility repair and Mamba-2 restart

- At the user's request, stopped the Mamba-2 seed-0 process. It had reached epoch 20, global step 4,170/51,360 (about 49% of that epoch), but terminated before an epoch-boundary checkpoint. The intact `latest.pt` remains epoch 19, global step 4,066; the old run metadata now records the interruption. No ViT or unrelated process was stopped.
- Loss diagnosis: overall mean train JEPA loss fell from **0.764172 (epoch 1)** to **0.340943 (epoch 19)**. Source losses moved differently: spectrogram and RFP declined, while WiFi CSI and NRPos Indoor increased. A read-only CUDA probe on the epoch-19 checkpoint found near-zero pairwise source-gradient cosine values (−0.035 to +0.041 across two batches/source), consistent with weakly aligned source objectives. This small probe is diagnostic evidence, not a complete gradient-interference study. The LR schedule matched the prose config; no optimizer or dataset weighting was changed.
- Found a concrete validation issue: each epoch resampled block masks and channel subsets, and `_evaluate` cycled shorter validation sources to match the longest loader. Therefore the validation loss and best epoch were not strictly comparable across epochs.
- Changed validation to use a fixed seed per source, reset mask-collator counters, isolate CPU/CUDA RNG state from training, and evaluate each held-out example once with sample-weighted aggregation. Validation loaders use zero workers for reproducibility. The training data mixture, masks for training, optimizer/schedule, effective batch, model, and HDF5 inputs are unchanged.
- Added `--reset-validation-selection` for resume after the validation metric changes. The new run uses a separate output directory and resumes model/optimizer/scheduler/sampler state from the preserved epoch-19 `latest.pt`; it clears the old stochastic validation selection so only the fixed-seed metric selects a checkpoint.
- Verification: `python3 -m py_compile` passed; `uv run pytest -q tests/test_pretrain_progress.py` reported **4 passed**; the four-source Mamba CUDA dry-run passed on `cuda:0` with predictor shape `(48, 28, 256)`; a real four-source CUDA validation smoke returned identical source losses on two repeated passes.
- The repaired continuation completed epoch 20 at global step 4,280. Mean train loss was **0.344455**, fixed-seed validation loss **0.350029**, throughput **54.006 source examples/s**, and peak allocated/reserved VRAM **2,025,555,968 / 3,076,521,984 bytes**. `best.pt`, `latest.pt`, and periodic `epoch-0020.pt` were saved; the new best-selection epoch is 20. The process continues with full pretraining and downstream evaluation still pending.
- Loaded the new `latest.pt` read-only and verified architecture `mamba2`, seed 0, code revision/branch, resolved config, epoch/global step, validation protocol, best/last validation values, 263 optimizer-state entries, both scheduler states, and all four sampler states. The checkpoint remains under the ignored sibling run directory.
- Restart command (foreground PTY, `pipefail`, persistent tee) was: `set -o pipefail; ./.venv/bin/python -u src/pretrain.py --config configs/mamba2_prose.yml --device cuda --seed 0 --output-dir ../runs/latent-wave/full_runs/mamba2_prose_seed0_deterministic_val --resume ../runs/latent-wave/full_runs/mamba2_prose_seed0/latest.pt --reset-validation-selection 2>&1 | tee -a ../runs/latent-wave/full_runs/mamba2_prose_seed0_deterministic_val/train_console.log`. It loaded the expected checkpoint (`completed_epoch=18`, `global_step=4066`) and started epoch 20.

Left the active process and its loaded code untouched; defer any training-loop optimization and bounded validation until a safe checkpoint boundary and matched protocol are available.

### Host-overhead diagnosis — 2026-10-08 03:17 MDT

The Mamba process remains active. At **03:17:35 MDT**, its log was at epoch **46**, step **9,680/51,360**, rolling train loss **0.317633**, last validation **0.331827**, LR **9.485e-4**, EMA **0.996754**, and ETA **192,455 s (~53h28m)**. The RTX 5070 Ti samples varied from **14% to 25%** utilization over this brief observation, with about **6,987/16,303 MiB** in use. The process used about **108% CPU**. `/proc/1149902/io` showed unchanged physical `read_bytes` across a 5-second sample (39.406 GB cumulative), while character reads rose only about 70 KB; this short sample cannot rule out intermittent or cached data-loader work.

Static inspection found a plausible host-dispatch/synchronization cost in `src/models/jepa.py::compute_loss_groups` and `src/pretrain_jepa.py`'s training loop: channel counts are generated on CUDA and `torch.unique(...).tolist()` transfers group IDs to Python; each group then checks finiteness and converts its detached loss to a Python float before backward. At microbatch size 8 there can be up to eight groups per source microbatch, and the four sources plus eight accumulation microbatches permit up to 256 group calls per optimizer update. This is an upper bound, not a measured group count. The single completed ViT epoch logged **196.64 source examples/s**, compared with **50.67 source examples/s** for Mamba epoch 45. The pattern makes Python dispatch and device-host synchronization a throughput hypothesis; no profiler has confirmed it, and it does not explain or establish the validation-loss trend. No code, checkpoint, or active process was changed. A future optimization should first aggregate scalar logging and finite checks, then preserve per-example channel-selection semantics while removing avoidable CUDA-to-Python grouping, with parity and throughput checks before any new full run.

The measured loss trend does show aggregate JEPA improvement, though it is not monotonic: on the fixed protocol, Mamba validation fell **5.2%** from epoch 20 to 45, and the best was epoch 43 (**0.328130**) before epochs 44–45 regressed to **0.331827**. Source behavior remains mixed. This is pretraining validation only; all downstream tasks remain unevaluated, so it cannot answer whether Mamba improves the task results.

At **03:20 MDT**, saved a reproducibility snapshot beside the active run at `../runs/latent-wave/full_runs/mamba2_prose_seed0_deterministic_val/runtime_source_snapshot.tar.gz`, with manifest `runtime_source_snapshot.json`. The archive contains the current `src/`, `configs/`, `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, and `.python-version`; it excludes datasets, checkpoints, and `.venv`. The manifest records branch `master`, HEAD `330e9ef5f6eb69e728ba43b3755036cffdd086cd`, the dirty-worktree state, and SHA-256 `c54232ea620a329efb54c552b969f43983ecac6b11eff92311a967fe980e7663`. ### Read-only host/GPU utilization diagnosis — 2026-10-08 04:10 MDT

Sampled the active Mamba process and GPU without attaching a profiler or changing the run. Five `top` samples at two-second intervals showed PID **1149902** using **100.5–110% CPU** (about one CPU core); overall CPU idle stayed around **94–95%** with **0% I/O wait**. Five `nvidia-smi dmon` samples showed GPU SM utilization at **18–20%** and memory-controller utilization at **3–6%**. At the contemporaneous log point, Mamba seed 0 was in epoch **49/240**, step **10,330/51,360**, rolling train loss **0.317205**, latest completed fixed validation **0.332120**, and ETA **190,214 s (~52h50m)**; device memory was about **6,988/16,303 MiB**.

The active source path has concrete host synchronization points: `compute_loss_groups` in `src/models/jepa.py` calls GPU `torch.unique(...).tolist()` for per-batch channel groups; `src/pretrain_jepa.py` checks `if not torch.isfinite(loss)` and converts each detached group loss with `float(loss.detach())` inside the group loop before backward. At microbatch size 8, the configured four source loaders and eight accumulation microbatches permit up to 256 group calls per optimizer update (upper bound, not observed count). The CPU/GPU sample supports a host-dispatch/synchronization bottleneck hypothesis, but does not attribute the measured utilization to these specific operations or prove that they explain the full throughput gap. Do not edit the loaded training source or restart the active job. Any optimization must be evaluated after the matched baseline with semantic/gradient parity and a bounded validation-only benchmark before full adoption.

### Shared fixed-validation code audit — 2026-10-08 06:15 MDT

Read-only inspection of `src/pretrain_jepa.py` confirms that `_build_loaders` derives each source's train/validation indices from `config.seed + source_index + 1`; the Mamba-2 and ViT configs use the same seed and ordered source list. `_evaluate` requires zero-worker validation loaders, resets each validation collator counter, and seeds CPU and CUDA RNGs from `config.seed + 10,000 + source_index` before evaluating each source. ### Read-only throughput code audit — 2026-10-08 07:08 MDT

Inspected `src/pretrain_jepa.py`, `src/datasets/pretrain.py`, and both prose configs while Mamba remained active. Each of the four source-specific training loaders uses batch size **8**, **4** persistent workers, and pinned memory. The training loop fetches and processes each source batch sequentially, accumulating gradients over **8** microbatches before one optimizer update; this yields the configured effective batch of **4 × 8 × 8 = 256** examples. The sequential small-microbatch workload is a plausible contributor to the observed low GPU utilization, but the code inspection does not prove it is the bottleneck. The data path is on `/mnt/c`; earlier system sampling showed high CPU idle and low I/O wait, so storage contention is also unconfirmed. No active code, config, process, or checkpoint was changed. Any throughput trial should wait until this run is complete and preserve the matched effective batch for both architectures.

### Read-only runtime bottleneck diagnosis — 2026-10-08 08:58 MDT

A 10-second `nvidia-smi dmon` sample measured **14–20%** SM use and **3–7%** memory-controller use. Over the same interval, `vmstat` showed **94–95%** CPU idle and **0%** I/O wait; the trainer used about **106%** CPU and its 16 data workers used at most **0.6%** each. The training source contains CUDA synchronization points: `torch.unique(channel_counts).tolist()` in channel grouping, followed by a Python finiteness condition and `float(loss.detach())` for each loss group. At **1,712** source microbatches per epoch across four loaders, `.tolist()` alone forces at least **6,848** device-to-host synchronizations per epoch; the per-group checks and scalar reads add more. This is a likely contributor to poor GPU occupancy and long epoch time, not a demonstrated cause of validation behavior. No code or active process was changed; a safe synchronization reduction needs an isolated short-run benchmark before use in future training.

At the 08:58 log snapshot, Mamba-2 seed 0 remained active at epoch **65/240**, step **13,830/51,360** (**134/214**), rolling train loss **0.316332**, latest completed validation **0.328923**, LR **8.7433e-4**, EMA **0.997077**, elapsed **65,146 s**, and ETA **176,785 s (~49h06m)**. PyTorch memory was **2.36/6.14 GiB** allocated/reserved; GPU use was **8,029/16,303 MiB** at **17%**. No additional GPU process was present.

## 2026-10-09 — Diagnosis and clean resume from epoch 128

- Following the user's stop request, sent SIGTERM to the verified Mamba seed-0 trainer. Its handler finished epoch **128**, saved `latest.pt`, wrote `status=stopped_after_epoch`, and exited cleanly. Epoch 128 ended at step **27,392** with train loss **0.266441049** and fixed-validation loss **0.305403809**. Validation rose **0.58%** versus epoch 127, so the best remains epoch **127** at **0.303631013**. Per-source validation at epoch 128: spectrogram **0.237123394**, RFP **0.114716156**, WiFi CSI **0.458185968**, NRPos Indoor **0.411589719**.
- Recomputed the fixed-validation trend from `train.jsonl` rows with the correct human-readable epoch indices. From epochs **20–127**, aggregate train loss fell **21.42%**, fixed-validation loss fell **13.26%**, and the OLS validation slope was **−0.00031442 loss/epoch**. This does not support a global training stall. The source-level exception is spectrogram: its training loss fell **14.00%**, while its fixed validation loss rose **25.50%**. RFP, WiFi CSI, and NRPos Indoor validation fell **9.95%**, **13.88%**, and **26.62%**, respectively.
- Reconstructed the exact spectrogram split used by the loader (seed **1**, 3,166 train / 166 validation samples). Per-image mean and standard-deviation summaries were broadly similar across splits: mean of image means **0.0060 train / 0.0447 validation**; median image standard deviation **0.3601 / 0.3553**. This rules out a large simple pixel-scale shift, but does not establish why spectrogram validation degrades.
- A read-only target-encoder diagnostic on the saved epoch-128 checkpoint found nonzero pooled feature spread in every source (mean per-dimension SD **0.3509** spectrogram, **0.5555** RFP, **0.2698** WiFi CSI, **0.1299** NRPos Indoor) and centered effective ranks **60.74**, **40.62**, **21.29** (42 WiFi examples), and **56.92** (128 examples). A real spectrogram batch produced finite nonzero student and predictor gradients (global L2 **0.1905** and **0.1982**) and no target-encoder gradients, as intended. No representation collapse, non-finite gradient, EMA, loss-alignment, or optimizer defect was found. Full outputs are in `../runs/latent-wave/full_runs/mamba2_prose_seed0_deterministic_val/diagnostic_20261009.log`.
- No speculative hyperparameter change was applied to the controlled prose run. Resumed the same config and seed from `latest.pt` with the exact command below; preflight passed with `(48, 28, 256)` on CUDA. At 04:09 MDT metadata records `running`, **128 completed epochs**, global step **27,392**, and best epoch **127**. The matched-seed queue was restarted after it correctly exited during the clean pause; it remains gated on full seed-0 completion and downstream outputs.
- The preserved ViT seed-0 console log confirms its earlier stop was a handled **SIGTERM after epoch 1**, not a Python exception. Its source checkpoint and fixed-validation resume copy were verified byte-identical. The guarded ViT handoff and seed-0 evaluation gate are now running again: the handoff will resume ViT with `--reset-validation-selection` only after Mamba reports 240 completed epochs, and evaluation remains gated until both full runs complete.

set -o pipefail
./.venv/bin/python -u src/pretrain.py --config configs/mamba2_prose.yml --device cuda --seed 0 --output-dir ../runs/latent-wave/full_runs/mamba2_prose_seed0_deterministic_val --resume ../runs/latent-wave/full_runs/mamba2_prose_seed0_deterministic_val/latest.pt 2>&1 | tee -a ../runs/latent-wave/full_runs/mamba2_prose_seed0_deterministic_val/train_console.log

## Throughput investigation — 2026-10-09 05:06 MDT

A read-only sample during Mamba epoch 132 found the configured HDF5 files reside on `/mnt/c` (`9p`/DrvFS). The active Mamba parent used about one CPU core, the recent GPU samples were **16–17%** utilization and **5,519 MiB** of **16,303 MiB**, and most DataLoader worker processes were sleeping. The completed Mamba epoch 131 ran at **48.704 source examples/s**, versus **196.643** in the earlier single ViT epoch. The loader does use four workers and pinned memory; the encoder uses official Mamba-2 modules in both directions. This is evidence of a throughput gap and a possible filesystem/CPU-side bottleneck, but it does not identify the bottleneck conclusively or establish an architecture-only speed ratio. No live process/config/checkpoint was changed and no speculative optimization was applied.

## Source-level validation diagnostic — 2026-10-09 06:43 MDT

Compared fixed-protocol Mamba seed-0 epochs 20 and 136 from the generated per-source trace:

| Pretraining source | Train-loss change | Validation-loss change |
|---|---:|---:|
| Spectrogram | −20.60% | **+24.13%** |
| RFP | −15.91% | −14.28% |
| WiFi CSI | −22.48% | −15.96% |
| NRPos indoor | −28.15% | −28.89% |

Code inspection found that `src/pretrain_jepa.py::_build_loaders` makes a deterministic, unlabeled 95/5 sample split independently within each configured source. `_evaluate` fixes the validation RNG and mask/channel sampling; `src/datasets/pretrain.py` reads the configured cached tensor without another source-specific transform. The inventory says the configured spectrogram cache is already transformed/normalized and has no labels or group IDs. This provides no evidence of a validation-preprocessing mismatch or confirmed implementation bug. The spectrogram train/validation divergence is consistent with a source-specific generalization gap or sample heterogeneity; its cause is unresolved. The active primary run is unchanged. Consider only bounded, single-factor validation tuning after the matched primary runs release the GPU; do not select via downstream test results.

## Training-loop performance audit — 2026-10-09 13:01 MDT

A brief read-only profiling pass while the active Mamba-2 run continued found the trainer process averaging about **104% CPU** over its lifetime. Its **16** DataLoader workers each showed only **0.2–0.6% CPU** and were mostly waiting in `poll_schedule_timeout`. Six one-second GPU samples showed **15–21% SM** and **2–7% memory-controller** utilization. Source inspection of `src/pretrain_jepa.py` found that the inner source/group loop converts each detached loss to a Python `float` and performs a tensor finiteness check on every group before backward. Host scalar conversion and boolean evaluation of CUDA tensors can force device synchronization; together with 1,712 microbatches per epoch across four sources, this is a plausible source of Python/kernel-launch overhead and low occupancy. This observation is suggestive, not a confirmed bottleneck diagnosis. No instrumentation was attached, no code/config/checkpoint was changed, and the active training process was left untouched. If an optimization is pursued later, accumulate detached metrics on-device and reduce the frequency of synchronization checks, then validate it on a controlled smoke run and apply the same trainer code to both architectures.

At the same poll, Mamba-2 seed 0 was active at epoch **157**, global step **33,510/51,360** (**126/214 optimizer steps**), with rolling train loss **0.240740232**, latest fixed-validation loss **0.283850867** (epoch 156), LR **2.96149e-4**, EMA **0.998610**, elapsed time **163,008 s**, and ETA **86,831 s (~24h07m)**. PyTorch allocated/reserved memory was **2.43/6.04 GiB**; the RTX 5070 Ti reported **7,945/16,303 MiB** at **15%** utilization. ### Throughput diagnostic and live poll — 2026-10-09 15:52 MDT

A 5-second read-only `nvidia-smi dmon` sample showed GPU SM utilization **16–20%**, memory-controller use **3–5%**, and power **74–75 W**. A simultaneous `ps` sample showed the trainer at **102% CPU** (about one core); its HDF5 DataLoader workers were mostly **0.2–0.6% CPU**. The training loop appears to pay repeated host/device synchronization: `compute_loss_groups` can produce several channel-count loss groups per batch, while `src/pretrain_jepa.py` checks `torch.isfinite(loss)` and converts each group loss to a Python float, then checks every parameter gradient individually with `torch.isfinite(...).all()` before stepping. These scalar decisions synchronize CUDA work. This is a likely throughput bottleneck, not evidence of an objective or gradient correctness bug; source HDF5 reads on `/mnt/c` remain another possible factor. The active run already has this code loaded, so it was not changed. Revisit aggregation/deferred scalar checks in a controlled performance-only run after the matched baseline, preserving the current run and its exact protocol.
